# Helix.AI Worklog

## 2026-10-07 — Platform P1: objective, persisted plan, execution intent, explicit approval

Plan: `.cursor/plans/noricum-scientific-orchestration-platform.md` §P1. Gated by `HELIX_SCIENCE_GATE_V1` (default off); `/execute` byte-identical with the flag off (verified by golden-path + user-flow integration tests and the `gate_off` unit test).

- `backend/orchestration/ledger.py` — the one write path (`record_objective/plan/intent/approval`, `load_*`, `records_by_trace`) → `sessions/{sid}/platform/{objectives,plans,intents,approvals}/`. Intents immutable, approvals write-once. P7 routes this through `KnowledgeStore`.
- `objective_builder.py` — mints `objective_id` + `trace_id` (the only origin of a loop's trace). Deterministic always; LLM enrichment behind `HELIX_OBJECTIVE_LLM=1`, never in mock mode, only touches question/system/evidence/outputs/criteria, falls back on any error.
- `plan_staging.py` — legacy plan dict (router IR or tabular plan) → `ScientificPlan` (hashed, versioned, `supersedes`) → `ExecutionIntent`; structured `RationaleItem[]` from router fields / tabular `goal`+`operation`, never prose.
- `intent_builder.py` — `provider_id=legacy:<Infra>`, `capability_id=local_compute:<tool>`, `input_manifest_hash` (URIs + size + sha256 for readable local files), `execution_parameters_hash`; refuses in `DENIED`/`WAITING_FOR_SECURITY_REVIEW`.
- `approval_service.py` — `decide_intent` is the single approval path for HTTP and chat: checks staged intent id, intent hash, plan hash, *and* re-hashes the live pending plan (a plan mutated after staging → 409 `plan_changed`); write-once per intent. `verify_approved_for_execution` is the broker pre-check (invariant `approval.execution_intent_hash == intent.execution_intent_hash`).
- `backend/api/approvals.py` — `POST /session/{sid}/intents/{iid}/approve|reject|request-changes`, `GET …/intents/pending`; 404 with gate off, 401 without `X-Helix-User`, 409 on hash/intent/double-decision mismatch. Approve → `READY_TO_EXECUTE`; execution still goes through `/execute` (P3A: fabric).
- `main.py` — staging hooks in both plan branches (`platform` block in response), chat approval recorded *before* `_autobind_plan_inputs` mutates arguments, pre-execution verify before tabular and broker execution, platform fields carried across `EXECUTING`/`WAITING_FOR_INPUTS` checkpoints; router included.
- `WorkflowCheckpoint` — optional `trace_id`, `objective_id`, `pending_plan_id/hash`, `pending_execution_intent_id/hash`, `approval_id`; serialised only when set (legacy checkpoints unchanged).
- `HandoffPolicy` — `AgentRole.HUMAN_APPROVAL`; `Infra/CodeGen → HumanApproval → Broker`; `approval_required=True` makes direct `→ Broker` illegal.
- Tests: +45 unit (objective, plan persistence, intent, approval endpoint + NL path, trace propagation, handoff) + 4 ORCH-001 steps un-skipped. Unit suite 967 pass / same 5 pre-existing failures. Evidence: `artifacts/test_results/p1_objective_plan_intent_approval_2026-10-07.md`.
- Known gap (for P3A): the intent covers the plan *as staged*; `_autobind_plan_inputs` fills missing inputs at approval time *after* the approval is recorded. P3A must re-derive `input_manifest_hash` after binding and either match the intent or require re-approval.
- Known gap (for P9): frontend does not yet read `platform`/`READY_TO_EXECUTE`; the approval card still uses the chat path.
- Observed, not fixed: `test_iterative_workflows_local` fails after `test_user_flows` in one process (state leakage via module-level `history_manager`), gate on or off.

## 2026-10-07 — Noricum platform gap analysis + incremental plan (Helix + DataWeaver)

Inspected both repos (Helix working tree at `2a062a3`; DataWeaver.AI shallow-cloned to `/tmp`, removed after) against the target cycle Objective → Plan → Security Gate → Infra Selection → Human Approval → Execution → Learning → Decision. No product code changed.

Deliverables: `docs/architecture/NORICUM_PLATFORM_GAP_ANALYSIS.md` (inventory, gap matrices for Helix §A–G + registry and DataWeaver §A–F, workflow-to-component mapping, decisions D1–D8) and `.cursor/plans/noricum-scientific-orchestration-platform.md` (Phases 0–9, flag-gated strangler plan with files/tests/exit criteria).

Key findings: Helix has the handoff chain, checkpoint state machine, approval staging, run/artifact ledger, infra decision contract and multi-backend executors, but no `ScientificObjective`, no persisted plan/approval records (approval is LLM-intent only), no security gate on scientific actions (only upload intake), a closed `Literal[Local|EC2|EMR|Batch|Lambda]` target set duplicated in three places, no provider adapter interface, and zero DataWeaver integration. DataWeaver has SQLAlchemy/Alembic (SQLite/Postgres), Design/Build/Test lineage hashes, `FileRelationship` edges with confidence, dataset matching and merge/QA/Plotly services, but in-memory session context, two duplicate DBTL schemas, two colliding `/api/bio` routers, no auth, and ~14 of the 21 required entities absent.

Baseline: `HELIX_MOCK_MODE=1 pytest tests/unit` → 876 passed / 5 failed / 1 skipped; failures pre-existing (3× approval classifier under mock mode, 1× S3 network, 1× sandbox import expectation). Recorded in `artifacts/test_results/unit_baseline_2026-10-07_platform_gap_analysis.md`. Plan Phase 1.3 (explicit approval endpoint) is expected to resolve the approval cluster.

Related issues noticed, not fixed (out of scope): orchestration duality (`agent.py` vs `orchestrator.py`), `main.py` at 7.5k LOC, uncommitted unrelated working-tree changes that blur test attribution.

## 2026-09-26 — Website: scientific use cases with maturity labels

Replaced `/use-cases` (previously four Helix workflow recipes) with four scientific use cases at a higher altitude: reproducible analyses (Helix.AI, available), fragmented data (DataWeaver.AI, in development), closed-loop DBTL and lab orchestration (both labelled *seeking design partners* with a collaboration CTA). ## 2026-05-17 — Tabular target ranking: sheet-aware execution + deterministic ratio

### Root cause (sessions `e69e18a8…`, beta tester workflow)
`execute_analysis_plan` loaded the upload-time default sheet (`gene_morpheus_mem`, first tab) while plans/code referenced `ts_final` with `median_tumor` / `max_median_gtex`. Sandbox returned `missing_required_columns` but API reported `tabular_analysis_execute_success`.

### Fix
- `backend/tabular_qa/sheet_selection.py`: resolve sheet from plan → command → profile; detect logical failures.
- `backend/tabular_qa/target_ranking.py`: deterministic `median_tumor / max_median_gtex` ranking when requested.
- `analysis_executor.py`: load correct sheet, run deterministic path, treat missing-column results as errors.
- `analysis_planner.py`: persist `plan["sheet"]` and refresh schema preview for that sheet.
- `main.py`: pass `original_command` into executor on approval.
- `command_router.py`: deterministic route for tumor/normal ranking prompts with in-session tabular upload.

### Tests
- `tests/unit/backend/test_tabular_target_ranking.py`

## 2026-05-11 — Advisory quick-reply UX fixes + smoke test suite

### Root cause (two bugs)
1. **Frontend buttons didn't submit**: `setCommand(opt)` is async; by the time
   `setTimeout(form.requestSubmit, 0)` fired, `command` state was still stale so
   `handleSubmit` bailed on `!command.trim()`.
2. **"yes, inspect it" → approval gate dead-end**: The approval classifier sent any
   affirmative-prefix phrase to the LLM even when `has_pending_plan=False`.  The LLM
   reliably returned "yes = approval", causing the backend to return "There is no
   pending workflow to approve" for every advisory quick-reply.

### Fix
- `frontend/src/App.tsx`: replaced `setCommand + form.requestSubmit` with direct
  `executeCommand(text)` call; `executeCommand` takes text as a parameter and also
  handles scroll-to-pending automatically.
- `backend/orchestration/approval_classifier.py`: gated both keyword fast-path and LLM
  path behind `has_pending_plan`.  When the session is idle, `is_approval_command`
  returns `False` immediately without any LLM call.

### Regression coverage added
- `tests/unit/backend/test_approval_classifier.py`: updated all tests to reflect
  corrected semantics; added `test_keyword_approvals_return_false_without_pending_plan`
  and `test_ambiguous_phrase_without_pending_plan_returns_false`.
- `tests/integration/test_user_flows.py::TestAdvisoryFollowup`: 7 new tests covering
  the full advisory → quick-reply → normal response path.

### Smoke suite
- `tests/smoke/ec2_smoke.py`: live end-to-end smoke script that hits the real backend.
  Covers: health, session creation, meta question, advisory quick-reply (3 phrases),
  bare "yes" with no pending plan, unknown intent.  Verified 6/6 PASS against EC2.
- `scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh`: smoke suite runs automatically post-deploy when
  `HELIX_SMOKE_URL` is set.

## 2026-05-11 — Slice A: Persistence contract for a turn

### Root cause
Multiple structural issues caused history, run records, and artifacts to be silently
dropped or misdirected:
1. `_materialize_run_artifacts`, `download_script`, and the script generation helper all
   used `Path(__file__).parent.parent / "sessions"` — a hardcoded path that diverges
   from `history_manager.storage_dir` whenever `HELIX_SESSIONS_DIR` is set.
2. The `execute_plan=True` branch in `/execute` called `build_standard_response` and
   `return CustomJSONResponse(standard)` directly, bypassing `_dispatch_result` and
   never writing a history entry for agent execute_plan turns.
3. The `qa_safe_fallback` path returned a raw `CustomJSONResponse` without going through
   `_dispatch_result`, so advisory/Q&A turns in mock mode also skipped history.

### Fix
- Extracted `_record_turn()` as the single authoritative persistence helper, called from
  `_dispatch_result` (the canonical exit point) and any branch that needs history writes.
- Replaced the inlined persistence block in `_dispatch_result` with a call to `_record_turn`.
- Fixed all three hardcoded `sessions` path references to use `history_manager.storage_dir`.
- Fixed `execute_plan=True` branch to route through `_dispatch_result`.
- Fixed `qa_safe_fallback` branch to route through `_dispatch_result`.
- Fixed `bio_orchestrator._script_path_for()` to use `history_manager.storage_dir`.

### Tests
`tests/unit/backend/test_turn_persistence_contract.py` — 9 cases:
- Scriptable bio tool (bulk_rnaseq_analysis): history+run record written; artifact URIs exist under storage_dir.
- Advisory / handle_natural_command: history entry written; ≤1 run record.
- execute_plan=True path: history entry written (was broken before).
- Multi-turn: N turns → N history entries in same session.

## 2026-05-10 — Remove broad length fallback from tabular analysis planning gate

### Root cause
`is_analytical_request` in `backend/tabular_qa/analysis_planner.py` contained a final
fallback: `return len(c.split()) >= 8`. Any message longer than 8 words on a session
with a tabular upload was classified as an executable analysis request, bypassing the
LLM router entirely. This caused meta/comparative questions ("what's the difference
between your next suggested step and the previous analysis?") to produce a full
`tabular_analysis` plan waiting for approval — the wrong response type.

### Fix
Removed the length fallback. `is_analytical_request` now returns `False` for anything
that does not match the explicit analytical cue keywords. Ambiguous or conversational
turns fall through to the main LLM router, which already has `tabular_qa`,
`handle_natural_command`, and `unknown_intent` available, giving us a real "I don't
know / please clarify" boundary without adding new keyword rules.

### Tests
`tests/unit/backend/test_analysis_planner_gate.py` — 27 cases covering:
- 16 meta/conversational/comparative commands that must NOT trigger the gate
- 10 analytically explicit commands that MUST trigger it
- no-upload guard



## 2026-05-09 — Tabular reproducibility bundle (inputs + run ledger)

- **Root cause:** `tabular_analysis` wrote plots under `uploads/plots/` and never
  persisted `analysis.py` or `produced_artifacts`, so `build_bundle` produced nearly
  empty ZIPs and used the wrong sessions root when `HELIX_SESSIONS_DIR` is set.
- **Fix:** On successful tabular execution, persist `analysis.py`, plot copy, and
  optional `tables/tabular_result.json` under `storage_dir/<session_id>/runs/<run_id>/`,
  register `produced_artifacts` + `tool_args` in history, return `run_id` / `script_path`
  in the API result. `build_bundle` uses `history_manager.storage_dir`, packs
  `inputs/raw/*` for `tabular_analysis`, and dedupes plot/table sweeps vs artifact URIs.
- **Order:** Tabular path calls `add_history_entry` before `build_standard_response`
  so artifact download links and `get_run` see the new ledger row.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/backend/test_bundle_generator_tabular.py`.
- **Evidence:** `artifacts/test_results/bundle_tabular_2026-05-09.txt`

## 2026-05-09 — Tabular codegen retries + retry UX

- **Config:** `HELIX_TABULAR_CODEGEN_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default 4, clamped 1–16) drives
  the analysis-plan executor loop and `run_tabular_qa` (`backend/tabular_qa/codegen_attempts.py`).
- **Diagnostics:** Failed runs return `attempts_used`, `attempts_max`, `error_class`,
  `last_code_preview`, and `tabular_retry_available` where applicable
  (`analysis_executor.py`, `agent.py`, `main.py` tabular approval path).
- **Recovery:** On tabular plan execution failure, the session is returned to
  `WAITING_FOR_APPROVAL` with the same staged plan so **Approve.** runs codegen again.
- **UI:** `App.tsx` renders tabular errors with attempt counts, optional code preview,
  and **Try again (re-run approval)** when `tabular_retry_available` is true.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/backend/test_codegen_attempts.py`.

## 2026-05-06 — Session `584fce12-7108-4698-a9f0-8826b8dfd1a8`: router ignored in-session Excel

### What happened (EC2 `/opt/helix/sessions/584fce12-…json`)
1. User uploaded `media-5 (2).xlsx` (~2.2 MB); intake and `schema_preview` (tabular,
   sheet `gene_morpheus_mem`, 90×67) succeeded.
2. First command `what can I do with this file?` → `tool=agent` → advisory JSON
   (correct), including a suggested follow-up to profile the workbook.
3. Second command `profile the Excel workbook now` → **`tool=unknown_intent`** with
   `needs_clarification`, claiming no workbook was provided — contradicting the
   session’s `uploaded_files`.

### Root cause
The LLM router (`CommandRouter._route_with_llm`) only saw the bare user text. It did
not receive explicit session upload metadata, so it treated the request as
underspecified and returned `unknown_intent` instead of `tabular_analysis` /
`tabular_qa`.

### Fix
1. **Deterministic pre-route** (`backend/command_router.py`): If the session already
   has a tabular upload and the command matches profiling/inspection verbs plus
   workbook/file language (or “profile/describe it”), route to `tabular_analysis`
   (same path that loads the file and returns schema / dimensions — appropriate
   “profile”).
2. **LLM router hint**: Append a short “Session context: tabular file(s) already
   uploaded …” line to the router user prompt when uploads exist, instructing not to
   use `unknown_intent` for generic “the workbook” follow-ups.
3. **Parameter hygiene**: `_extract_parameters` for tabular tools now prefers
   `local_path` over bare `filename`; `dispatch_tool` tabular handler accepts
   `file_path` as an alias for `data_path` (`backend/main.py`).

### Verification
- Unit test `test_profile_workbook_with_session_xlsx_routes_tabular` in
  `tests/unit/backend/test_tabular_ingest.py`.

---

## 2026-05-05 — Fix: tabular analysis sandbox import error + history not saved on failure

### Symptoms (session `a189d1b5-362d-49b2-ad69-9c4c9ed11ae0`)
1. User ran a tabular analysis and received no output; session history was blank.
2. EC2 backend logs showed `KeyError: '__import__'` during sandboxed code execution.

### Root cause — Bug 1: `KeyError: '__import__'` opaque error / bad retry
`backend/tabular_qa/executor.py` runs LLM-generated code in a restricted dict-based
builtins environment that deliberately omits `__import__`. When the LLM produced
`import scipy` the sandbox raised `KeyError: '__import__'` — an internal Python
detail that is meaningless to the model. The retry in `analysis_executor.py` appended
that raw error to the END of the prompt, which the model largely ignored.

### Fix — Bug 1
1. **`backend/tabular_qa/executor.py`**: Detect `KeyError: '__import__'` and replace it
   with a human-readable (and LLM-readable) `SandboxImportError` message that names
   the pre-bound variables and explicitly forbids `import` lines.
2. **`backend/tabular_qa/analysis_executor.py`**: On retry, prepend a `CORRECTION
   REQUIRED` preamble that restates the constraint before the analysis goal, so the
   model reads the correction instruction first.

### Root cause — Bug 2: history never saved for tabular analysis path
`main.py` lines 2969-2982 (tabular analysis execution path) called
`return CustomJSONResponse(std)` without ever calling
`history_manager.add_history_entry`. Every other execution path reaches that call
via `_dispatch_result`, but this block was an early-return that bypassed it entirely,
leaving the session history empty regardless of success or failure.

### Fix — Bug 2
Added an explicit `history_manager.add_history_entry(...)` call in the tabular
analysis execution block in `backend/main.py`, immediately before the early
`return CustomJSONResponse(std)`. The call is wrapped in a try/except so a logging
failure can never mask the real result returned to the frontend.

### Files changed
- `backend/tabular_qa/executor.py` — translate `KeyError: '__import__'` → clear message
- `backend/tabular_qa/analysis_executor.py` — prepend error preamble on retry
- `backend/main.py` — write history entry before early return in tabular exec path

## 2026-05-06 — Beta hotfix: `No module named 'duckdb'` on file upload

### Symptom
User uploaded `media-5__2_.xlsx` to https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com/
(session `fd3e53f0-6ad2-4375-b8a5-70fea4535496`) and the backend returned
`No module named 'duckdb'`. Same root cause would have surfaced as
`No module named 'openpyxl'` once duckdb was installed, since the file is
`.xlsx` and `pandas.read_excel` requires openpyxl for that extension.

### Root cause
Deploy-contract gap, not a code bug. `backend/ds_pipeline/pipelines/ingest.py`
imports `duckdb` at file-upload time and uses `pandas.read_excel` (→ openpyxl)
for `.xlsx` inputs. Both deps were declared only in the **root**
`requirements.txt` (lines 26 and 54), but `scripts/ec2/bootstrap-ec2.sh:102`
and `scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh:25` both `pip install -r backend/requirements.txt`
— so the production `.venv` (a conda env at /opt/helix/.venv, Python 3.11)
never received them. Direct probe on EC2:

```
$ /opt/helix/.venv/bin/python -c "import duckdb"
ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'duckdb'
$ /opt/helix/.venv/bin/python -c "import openpyxl"
ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'openpyxl'
```

The split between the two requirements files has existed for a while; the bug
only became user-visible when this user happened to upload an Excel file
(triggering both imports).

### Fix
1. **Source of truth** (`backend/requirements.txt`): added `duckdb>=1.0` and
   `openpyxl>=3.1` under a comment explaining they're required by the
   ds_pipeline tabular-ingest path. This is what bootstrap and
   update-from-git both consume.
2. **Live remediation**: installed both into the running prod venv via
   `aws ssm send-command`:
   `sudo -u helix /opt/helix/.venv/bin/pip install duckdb>=1.0 openpyxl>=3.1`.
   Resolved to `duckdb-1.5.2`, `openpyxl-3.1.5`, `et-xmlfile-2.0.0`.
3. **Restart**: `systemctl restart helix-backend` so the running uvicorn
   process loads the new dependencies. `helix-backend` reports `active`,
   `/health` returns `status: healthy`.
4. **End-to-end replay**: re-ran `ingest_tabular` against the user's exact
   uploaded path
   `/opt/helix/sessions/fd3e53f0-6ad2-4375-b8a5-70fea4535496/uploads/raw/media-5__2_.xlsx`
   on the live backend. Result: 90 rows × 67 cols on sheet `gene_morpheus_mem`
   ingested cleanly.

### Why our existing tests didn't catch this
`tests/unit/backend/test_tabular_ingest.py` already exercises this exact import
path. It passes in dev because the developer venv was bootstrapped from the
root `requirements.txt` (which has duckdb), not from `backend/requirements.txt`.
The gate suites therefore measure the dev environment's deps, not the
production deploy contract. To prevent recurrence we should add a CI step that
provisions a clean venv from `backend/requirements.txt` and asserts
`from backend.main import app` and `from backend.ds_pipeline.pipelines.ingest
import ingest_tabular` both succeed. Tracked as a follow-up
(`backend-deploy-contract-not-tested`, see release_readiness.json blockers).

### Operational note
This is the second deploy-contract gap surfaced against the live beta in 24h
(prior was AL2 having no Node/npm). Both point at the same underlying issue:
the production deploy path is implicitly different from `pytest` on a developer
laptop. The deploy-script scripts assume a contract that's never end-to-end
verified. Recommendation: add a Makefile/CI target that builds a fresh venv
from `backend/requirements.txt` only, then imports the canonical entry points,
and gate releases on it.

## 2026-05-05 — Beta deploy: cleared `beta-bundle-mixed-content`

### What was deployed
- Pushed commits `865b892..55cd351` (4 commits from 2026-05-04 gate run) to `origin/main`.
- EC2 instance `i-0cc452521209457d4` (us-west-1, public IP `54.176.128.13`,
  user-facing DNS `helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com`) git-pulled to HEAD `55cd351`
  via `aws ssm send-command`.
- Built SPA locally with `VITE_API_BASE_URL=` (same-origin), shipped
  `frontend/dist` to EC2 via S3 presigned URL, atomically swapped into
  `/opt/helix/frontend/dist`. New bundle hash: `index-Do3-fXHQ.js`.
- Verified externally:
  - `curl https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com/` → 200 OK, references new bundle.
  - `curl https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com/health` → `{"status":"healthy",...}`.
  - `grep -c helix--Publi` on the deployed bundle → 0.

### Why the documented deploy path didn't work and what I did instead
- Port 22 is firewalled on the EC2 security group, so `ssh ec2-user@helix-beta…`
  is unavailable. Switched to AWS SSM (`aws ssm send-command`), matching the
  pattern in `scripts/aws/push-via-s3.sh` and `scripts/aws/build-on-ec2.sh`.
- `./scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh` failed at the `npm run build` step because
  Node/npm are **not installed on this AL2 instance at all** —
  `find / -maxdepth 5 -name npm` returned nothing. The bootstrap script
  acknowledges that AL2's old glibc means Node has to be built from source
  (~hours), and that build step has clearly never been completed on this host.
  Whoever previously deployed the SPA was building locally and shipping
  `dist/` — same workaround used here.
- EC2 instance role does not have read access to `s3://helix-build-794270057041/`
  (HEAD returned 403). Used a presigned URL instead so the EC2 download is
  plain HTTPS without IAM involvement.
- Pre-existing mixed `root`/`helix` ownership on `/opt/helix` was tripping
  git's "dubious ownership" check. Resolved by `chown -R helix:helix /opt/helix`
  and `git config --global --add safe.directory /opt/helix` for the helix user.

### Operational issues to fix before next deploy (not blocking this release)
1. **AL2 deploy contract is implicit.** `scripts/ec2/bootstrap-ec2.sh` and
   `scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh` assume `npm` is available on the host, but
   on this instance it isn't and never has been. Either:
   - install Node/npm on the host (NodeSource RPM doesn't work on AL2 due to
     glibc; would need to actually run the bootstrap's "build from source" leg),
   - migrate the host to AL2023 (newer glibc, NodeSource RPM works), or
   - formalise the off-host build contract (build on CI / dev machine, ship
     `dist/` via S3 + SSM). The third option is what's actually been
     happening de facto and matches `scripts/aws/push-via-s3.sh`.
2. **`/opt/helix` ownership drift.** Pre-existing mixed `root`/`helix`
   ownership broke `update-from-git.sh`. Add an idempotent
   `chown -R helix:helix /opt/helix` to the bootstrap and to a sanity-check
   step at the top of `update-from-git.sh`, and add `safe.directory /opt/helix`
   to the bootstrap's `helix` gitconfig.
3. **EC2 IAM role lacks read on `helix-build-794270057041`** even though both
   live in account 794270057041. Either grant the role or pick a single
   canonical relay bucket (`helix-ai-frontend-…` already accessible) and use
   it consistently in the deploy scripts.

### Release gate result for HEAD = `55cd351`
- The 2026-05-04 gate run measured the exact bytes that are now at HEAD
  (the gate ran against the post-fix working tree which has since been
  committed verbatim). Per workspace rule "release-readiness requires fresh
  benchmark and test results", that gate run is treated as the gate run for
  HEAD: no product-code drift between the measurement and HEAD, only commit
  metadata and one operational deploy.
- Both readiness blockers cleared:
  - `beta-bundle-mixed-content` → cleared by today's deploy (above).
  - `uncommitted-changes` → cleared by `git push origin 7a41660..55cd351`.
- `readiness` flipped to `true` in `artifacts/release_readiness.json`.

| benchmark_50_workflows | 50/50 (p50=15.8s, p95=19.2s) | ≥ 0.95 | PASS |

Full breakdown in `artifacts/release_readiness.json`.

### Root-cause fixes made during the gate run (in scope, not silent broadening)

1. **`backend/main.py`** — `_should_clear_pending_plan_after_execution()`'s status
   allow-list was missing `pipeline_executed`, the synchronous-success status
   `CommandProcessor.execute_pipeline()` returns when the planned pipeline has
   zero remaining steps. Production effect: a successful zero-step early-return
   would leave the session's pending plan dangling, breaking the next turn in
   the demo1 / approve→rerun flow. Added regression test
   `test_pipeline_executed_status_clears_pending_plan` in
   `tests/unit/backend/test_phase25_benchmark_turn_fixtures.py`.

2. **`tests/integration/test_execute_five_demo_golden_paths.py`** — the
   demo1 assertion at line 153 still allowed only `{"success", "pipeline_submitted"}`
   for the post-approval status; it now also accepts `pipeline_executed`.

3. **`tests/workflows/test_e2e_multi_step_workflows.py`** —
   `test_handle_command_routes_to_workflow_handler` mocked the multi-step
   handler and `_get_agent` but not `classify_intent`, which raises by design
   in `HELIX_MOCK_MODE` (see `backend/intent_classifier.py:52`). Added a third
   `patch('backend.intent_classifier.classify_intent', ...)` to the same `with`
   block, mirroring the autouse fixture in `tests/unit/backend/conftest.py`.

4. **`tests/demo_scenarios/framework/tracer.py`** — added
   `from __future__ import annotations` so the PEP 604 union `AgentRole | str`
   on line 75 doesn't blow up at class-definition time under Python 3.9. The
   repo officially targets `>=3.10` (`tests/demo_scenarios/pyproject.toml`,
   EC2 bootstrap installs `python3.11`), but the local `.venv` is 3.9.6, so
   without the future-import the entire `tests/unit/test_semantic_invariants.py`
   collection failed.

5. **`tests/evals/conftest.py`** (new) — evals MUST exercise the live LLM
   router (otherwise they're not testing routing quality). The repo-level
   `tests/conftest.py` setdefaults `HELIX_MOCK_MODE=1` for safety, which makes
   `command_router._route_with_llm` and `intent_classifier._classify_intent_with_llm`
   raise. New eval-scoped conftest (a) loads `.env`, (b) flips
   `HELIX_MOCK_MODE=0`, (c) cleanly skips the suite when no
   `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`/`AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY` is set so CI
   without secrets stays green instead of producing 70+ "LLM unavailable"
   failures.

6. **`frontend/e2e/visual.spec.ts` + regenerated snapshots** — the empty-state
   conversation pane no longer contains "Run a command to see your prompts here";
   that placeholder was replaced by `CapabilityGrid` (see
   `frontend/src/components/CapabilityGrid.tsx:4-7`). Updated the locator to
   anchor on `Here's what Helix.AI can do — click any item to try it:`.
   Visual snapshots regenerated to absorb the May 2 → May 4 demoScenarios.ts
   growth (+513 LOC → taller landing page).

### Router-quality regressions (do not block release; track for GA)

Live-LLM eval failures, all from `bio.router.*`:

| ID | Got | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| `router.plasmid.default_vector` | `mutate_sequence` | `plasmid_visualization` |
| `bio.router.plasmid.insert_default_vector` | `mutate_sequence` | `plasmid_visualization` |
| `bio.router.directed_evolution.parse_parameters` | `mutate_sequence` | `directed_evolution` |
| `bio.router.quality_assessment.from_inline_fasta` | `fastqc_quality_analysis` | `quality_assessment` |
| `bio.router.plan.trim_merge_qc` | …`fastqc_quality_analysis` (tail) | …`quality_assessment` (tail) |
| `bio.router.clustering.plain_cluster_word_no_fasta_no_route` | `clustering_analysis` | `bulk_rnaseq_analysis` |

Two clusters: (a) plasmid/directed-evolution prompts being eaten by
`mutate_sequence`, (b) `fastqc_quality_analysis` shadowing `quality_assessment`.
Both stem from commit `c1e1e18` ("feat(router): harden routing with
deterministic pre-router, strict schema, split fallbacks"). Likely fixes:

- Tighten the LLM router prompt's tool-disambiguation section for plasmid vs
  mutate_sequence (add a "if user mentions a vector / insert / plasmid map →
  plasmid_visualization" rule).
- Add a deterministic pre-router rule: plain `cluster` (no FASTA, no count
  matrix) → no-route (let the agent ask).

### Blockers preventing readiness=true

- **`beta-bundle-mixed-content`** (high) — see investigation note below.
- **`uncommitted-changes`** (medium) — gate was run against the dirty tree;
  fixes must be committed and the gate re-confirmed against HEAD.

## 2026-05-04 — Investigation: "Server: error" on helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com

### Symptom
The deployed beta UI at `https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com/` shows a red
`Server: error` badge. The frontend never reaches a healthy state, and (by
extension) every other API call from the SPA fails too.

### Root cause
The JS bundle currently served by nginx
(`/assets/index-DnhrdT75.js`) was built with a baked-in
`VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://helix--Publi-7CO3kaz1lrRR-443122446.us-west-1.elb.amazonaws.com`
(visible in the minified bundle as
`Ml=iG("http://helix--Publi-7CO3kaz1lrRR-443122446.us-west-1.elb.amazonaws.com")??""`).

That URL has two fatal problems for a browser loading the page over HTTPS:

1. **Mixed active content** — `axios.get('http://...')` from an `https://` page
   is blocked unconditionally by Chrome/Firefox/Safari, so
   `helixApi.healthCheck()` rejects → `setServerStatus('error')`
   (`frontend/src/App.tsx:305-317`).
2. **Internal AWS hostname** — the ALB DNS name `helix--Publi-...elb.amazonaws.com`
   does not resolve from the public internet (`curl` from outside AWS returns
   `Could not resolve host`), so even without the mixed-content block the
   request would fail.

The backend itself is healthy. Same-origin probing confirms it:

```
$ curl -i https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com/health
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
{"status":"healthy","service":"Helix.AI Bioinformatics API","agent_disabled":false,"mock_mode":false}
```

The EC2 nginx config (`scripts/ec2/nginx-helix.conf.template`) already proxies
`/health`, `/execute`, `/agent`, `/create_session`, `/download`, `/mcp/*`,
`/session/*`, `/jobs/*` to `127.0.0.1:8001`, and the EC2 install/update scripts
(`scripts/ec2/bootstrap-ec2.sh:109`, `scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh:41`) build
the SPA with `VITE_API_BASE_URL=` (empty) so it uses same-origin. So whatever
bundle is in `frontend/dist` on the host was NOT produced by those scripts —
someone built it locally with the wrong env var (matching the snippet in
`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_EXPLANATION.md:146,169`) and rsync'd/scp'd it
onto the host.

### Fix (not applied — investigation only)
On the EC2 host (`/opt/helix/Helix.AI` or wherever `HELIX_ROOT` points), run:

```bash
cd <HELIX_ROOT>
HELIX_SKIP_PIP=1 ./scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh
# or, if you only want to rebuild the frontend without restarting the backend:
cd frontend && npm ci && VITE_API_BASE_URL= npm run build
sudo systemctl reload nginx
```

After that, reload `https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com/` — the badge should
turn green ("Server: healthy") and `/create_session`, `/execute`, `/agent`
should all start working again.

### Related issues to clean up (not blocking the immediate fix)
- `docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_EXPLANATION.md:146,169` — hardcodes the
  internal HTTP ALB URL as the build-time `VITE_API_BASE_URL`. This is what
  led to the bad bundle being produced in the first place. Either remove the
  snippet or rewrite it to use `VITE_API_BASE_URL=` (same-origin) for the EC2
  beta deployment, or `VITE_API_BASE_URL=https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com`
  for an explicit absolute build.
- Consider a CI/build-time guard that fails the frontend build when
  `VITE_API_BASE_URL` is set to an `http://` URL (mixed-content trap), and a
  release-readiness check that asserts the deployed bundle's API base resolves
  publicly over HTTPS (or is empty for same-origin).

## 2026-04-27 — UX + advisory schema + agent.md fix

### Summary
Completed the Plan → Approve → Execute UX loop with consistent advisory rendering
and canonical JSON schema across all 50 bioinformatics workflow types.

### Changes
1. **Frontend UX** (`frontend/src/App.tsx`, `theme.css`)
   - Immediate "pending" placeholder + rotating bioinformatics loading messages
   - Follow-up chips now auto-submit (executeCommand) instead of pre-filling
   - Advisory JSON detection handles raw JSON, fenced JSON, all legacy shapes
   - `renderAdvisoryJSON` rewritten for canonical `HelixAdvisory` schema
   - Prompt bubble uses same font/size as response text

2. **Canonical Advisory Schema** (`backend/advisory_normalizer.py`, `backend/main.py`)
   - New Pydantic `HelixAdvisory` model: title, summary, classification, sections,
     workflow_steps, requirements, questions_for_user, next_steps
   - `normalize_advisory_text()` normalises 3 observed LLM output shapes into one
   - `main.py` calls normalizer on every text response before returning to frontend

3. **Agent prompt fix** (`backend/agent.py`)
   - AGENT_PROMPT_PATH now searches [repo_root/agent.md, docs/agent.md]
   - Was silently falling back to a 2-line stub — root cause of poor response quality

4. **docs/agent.md §11** — updated to require canonical HelixAdvisory JSON schema

5. **nginx** — /docs /redoc /openapi.json now password-protected

6. **.env** — consolidated root + backend/.env; corrected stale API key; all model
   vars set (gpt-4.1 reasoning, gpt-4.1-mini routing)

7. **scripts/run_benchmark.py** — new 50-workflow benchmark runner (fresh session per
   probe, 120s timeout, structured JSON output to artifacts/)

### Benchmark result (run: 2026-04-27T23:05:00Z, commit dd36eaa)
- PASSED: 49/50  FAILED: 0  ERRORS: 1 (cold-start timeout, not a product bug)
- Canonical advisory rendering: 32/50 probes
- Plain text (specialist tools): 17/50 probes
- Latency p50 ≈ 25s, p95 ≈ 35s

### Root causes resolved
- Empty/minimal advisory responses: agent.py was loading 2-line fallback prompt
- Inconsistent JSON shapes: advisory_normalizer handles all observed LLM variants
- Double JSON rendering on re-submit: strip ```json``` fences before parse
- Follow-up chips not auto-submitting: onSelect now calls executeCommand directly
- 401 API key error: stale sk-svcacct- key in root .env replaced with sk-proj- key

Carries over from May 2 baseline.', 'resolution': 'Install R runtime in CI/CD environment (no impact on Python-only release path).'}, {'id': 'router-quality-regressions-may4', 'severity': 'low', 'description': "6 of 79 router evals regressed on the live LLM run on 2026-05-04. Plasmid/directed-evolution prompts misroute to mutate_sequence (3 cases); quality_assessment is shadowed by fastqc_quality_analysis (2 cases); plain 'cluster' word routes to clustering_analysis instead of bulk_rnaseq_analysis (1 case). Eval pass rate (92.4%) still meets the 90% threshold so this does not block release, but the regressions stem from the recent router rewrite (commit c1e1e18) and should be addressed in a follow-up before promoting beta to GA.", 'resolution': "Triage in worklog 2026-05-04 § 'Router-quality regressions'. Likely fix is tightening the LLM router's tool-disambiguation prompt for plasmid vs mutate_sequence and adding a deterministic pre-router rule for plain 'cluster' (no FASTA) → no-route."}, {'id': 'al2-deploy-contract-implicit', 'severity': 'medium', 'description': "scripts/ec2/bootstrap-ec2.sh and scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh assume Node/npm is installed on the EC2 host, but on the live beta box (Amazon Linux 2, glibc 2.26) it isn't and never has been: find / -maxdepth 5 -name npm returns nothing. Production deploys have implicitly relied on building the SPA off-host and shipping dist/ — same path used today. The documented deploy script will fail at the npm run build step on any AL2 host. Operational only; no impact on release behaviour.", 'resolution': "Either (a) install Node on AL2 via the bootstrap's build-from-source path (slow, fragile), (b) migrate to AL2023 with newer glibc so NodeSource RPMs work, or (c) formalise the off-host build contract (build on CI / dev machine, ship dist/ via S3 + SSM) and remove the npm step from the on-host scripts. (c) is what is actually happening; recommend it. See worklog 2026-05-05 § 'Operational issues to fix before next deploy'."}]

Likely fix is tightening the LLM router's tool-disambiguation prompt for plasmid vs mutate_sequence and adding a deterministic pre-router rule for plain 'cluster' (no FASTA) → no-route."}, {'id': 'al2-deploy-contract-implicit', 'severity': 'medium', 'description': "scripts/ec2/bootstrap-ec2.sh and scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh assume Node/npm is installed on the EC2 host, but on the live beta box (Amazon Linux 2, glibc 2.26) it isn't and never has been: find / -maxdepth 5 -name npm returns nothing. Production deploys have implicitly relied on building the SPA off-host and shipping dist/ — same path used today. The documented deploy script will fail at the npm run build step on any AL2 host. Operational only; no impact on release behaviour.", 'resolution': "Either (a) install Node on AL2 via the bootstrap's build-from-source path (slow, fragile), (b) migrate to AL2023 with newer glibc so NodeSource RPMs work, or (c) formalise the off-host build contract (build on CI / dev machine, ship dist/ via S3 + SSM) and remove the npm step from the on-host scripts. (c) is what is actually happening; recommend it. See worklog 2026-05-05 § 'Operational issues to fix before next deploy'."}]

## 2026-05-11 — Slice D: End-to-end flow tests (test_user_flows.py)

### What was done
Created `tests/integration/test_user_flows.py` covering all five user-visible archetypes:
- **Tabular profile**: upload → "profile the workbook" → summary text in `result.text`, history entry written.
- **Advisory**: "what can I do with this dataset?" → `result.advisory` populated with structured field, `result.text` is prose not raw JSON.
- **Unknown intent**: "potato" → `unknown_intent` clarification card.
- **Meta question after run**: after a bio run, a comparative Q&A question returns advisory text, NOT a new plan, and `workflow_state` is IDLE.
- **Tabular planning**: analytical command with tabular context routes to `tabular_analysis_plan` for approval.

### Root cause (advisory test fix)
The integration conftest classifies "what…" commands as `intent="qa"` via `_classify_intent`, routing them to the `qa_safe_fallback` path which uses a hardcoded string and bypasses `dispatch_tool` entirely. Fix: patch `classify_intent` inside `test_advisory_sets_structured_field` to return `"execute"`.

### Tests
`tests/integration/test_user_flows.py` — 10 tests, all passing.

---

## 2026-05-11 — Slice E: Session schema version + Reset UX

### What was done
- **`backend/history_manager.py`**: Added `CURRENT_SESSION_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1` constant. New sessions now include `schema_version: CURRENT_SESSION_SCHEMA_VERSION` in their stored JSON.
- **`frontend/src/App.tsx`**:
  - `handleNewSession` now additionally clears `previewTables`, `activities`, `pendingScenarioId`, and dismisses any schema version banner.
  - On session restore at startup, if the loaded session's `schema_version` is absent (< 1), a dismissible banner is shown: "This session was created on an older Helix version; some features (e.g. bundle downloads) may behave differently."

### Tests
`tests/unit/backend/test_session_schema_version.py` — 4 tests: constant type/value, in-memory field, on-disk persistence, legacy-session compatibility. All pass. 30-test suite clean.

Likely fix is tightening the LLM router's tool-disambiguation prompt for plasmid vs mutate_sequence and adding a deterministic pre-router rule for plain 'cluster' (no FASTA) → no-route."}, {'id': 'al2-deploy-contract-implicit', 'severity': 'medium', 'description': "scripts/ec2/bootstrap-ec2.sh and scripts/ec2/update-from-git.sh assume Node/npm is installed on the EC2 host, but on the live beta box (Amazon Linux 2, glibc 2.26) it isn't and never has been: find / -maxdepth 5 -name npm returns nothing. Production deploys have implicitly relied on building the SPA off-host and shipping dist/ — same path used today. The documented deploy script will fail at the npm run build step on any AL2 host. Operational only; no impact on release behaviour.", 'resolution': "Either (a) install Node on AL2 via the bootstrap's build-from-source path (slow, fragile), (b) migrate to AL2023 with newer glibc so NodeSource RPMs work, or (c) formalise the off-host build contract (build on CI / dev machine, ship dist/ via S3 + SSM) and remove the npm step from the on-host scripts. (c) is what is actually happening; recommend it. See worklog 2026-05-05 § 'Operational issues to fix before next deploy'."}]

## 2026-05-20 — tabular_qa demo: upload-CSV flow

### Context
Investigated session `553affd8-772c-4345-9fd0-4cd6b27d8cae` on EC2 (helix-beta):
- User pasted a DE results CSV inline in the command text → routed to generic `agent` → `classification.task_type: tabular_qa`
- Session completed correctly (1 turn, no errors, produced ranked gene table artifact)
- Root issue: no demo scenario existed for tabular_qa; inline-CSV was the only demonstrated path

### Changes
- `frontend/src/data/demoScenarios.ts`: added 6th scenario `tabular-qa-tcell-targets` (pan-cancer T cell DE table, `expectedBehavior: needs_inputs`, `followUpPrompt` uses `file_path: s3://noricum-ngs-data/demo/tabular_qa/pan_cancer_tcell_de_results.csv`). Updated keyword maps in both `getDemoScenarioByCommandAndTool` and `getDemoScenarioByCommand`.
- `frontend/src/components/DemoScenariosPanel.tsx`: grid updated to 6 columns (minWidth 1060px), badge/subtitle updated to "6 scenarios / 6 real bioinformatics workflows".
- `tests/integration/demo_e2e_probe.py`: added `_DEMO_DE_CSV` (13-gene pan-cancer T cell checkpoint DE CSV), `_upload_csv_to_session()` multipart upload helper, and `demo6.tabular_qa` entry that uploads CSV before question (no inline data in prompt). Main loop handles optional `upload_csv` step.
- `tests/integration/router_live_probe.py`: added `tabular_qa.de_question` and `tabular_qa.rank_genes` cases.

### Remaining action (manual)
Stage the demo CSV on S3 before the frontend followUpPrompt will work end-to-end:
  s3://noricum-ngs-data/demo/tabular_qa/pan_cancer_tcell_de_results.csv

## 2026-07-03 — Live beta pre-invite verification (BLOCKER found)
- Goal: verify all demos/examples on https://helix-beta.noricum-biosoft.com before sending beta invites.
- Added env-gated basic-auth (HELIX_BASIC_AUTH) support to tests/integration/router_live_probe.py and demo_e2e_probe.py (probes predate nginx basic auth).
- Router probe: 18/22. E2E probe: 4/9.
- ROOT CAUSE: OpenAI key on beta backend is out of quota — every LLM call returns 429 insufficient_quota. `_get_llm()` picks OpenAI when OPENAI_API_KEY is set with no runtime DeepSeek fallback, so the whole beta is non-functional (classifier phase fails before routing).
- BLOCKER: do NOT send invites. Fix = restore OpenAI quota OR switch beta to DeepSeek in /etc/helix/helix.env + restart, then re-run probes.
- Also fixed stale probe expectation: tabular_analysis is a valid route for rank/top-N table ops.
- Details: artifacts/failure_summaries/2026-07-03_beta_openai_quota_exhausted.md, artifacts/release_readiness_beta_live_2026-07-03.json

## 2026-07-03 (cont.) — Fix demo3 amplicon 10-min dead-end
- Root cause: should_stage_for_approval() returned False on has_execute_intent=True BEFORE the multi_step_workflow rule. Imperative "Run FastQC, trim adapters, merge..." → execute intent → gate skipped → composite multi_step_workflow fell through to tool-generator 3-attempt retry loop (~10 min) → empty text → "_build_actionable_fallback_text" message.
- Fix: backend/orchestration/approval_policy.py — moved the multi_step_workflow rule ABOVE the classifier call and has_execute_intent early-return. Composite workflows now ALWAYS stage a plan card first (Plan→Approve→Execute), decided structurally (no LLM call, robust to classifier failure).
- Tests: tests/unit/backend/test_staging_policy_qa_bypass.py — added 3 regression tests (staged despite execute intent; structural/no-classifier; approval still bypasses). 34 passed. Pre-existing unrelated failure in test_orchestration_modules.py::test_approval_policy_is_action_based (is_approval_command('Approve.') without has_pending_plan → False; not touched by this change).
- Deployed approval_policy.py to /opt/helix on beta (backup made), restarted helix-backend. Verified live:
  * amplicon initial → __plan__ / WAITING_FOR_APPROVAL / approval_required in ~5s (was ~10 min).
  * amplicon approve → pipeline_executed in ~2s (no dead-end).
- NOTE (drift): /opt/helix is a non-git tarball deploy; the fix is live but NOT yet committed to git. Commit backend/orchestration/approval_policy.py + the test so a fresh S3/tarball redeploy doesn't lose it.
- FOLLOW-UP (separate, lower priority): approved multi_step plan with no inputs reports "Pipeline Executed / All steps completed" — mildly misleading per scientific-integrity rule; and plan card shows a single generic "step1 (multi_step_workflow)" rather than decomposed FastQC/trim/merge steps.

## 2026-07-25 — Biosecurity landscape research (no code changes)
- Produced `docs/research/biosecurity-landscape.md`: problem landscape, existing solution ecosystem (SecureDNA / IBBIS Common Mechanism / Aclid, CASPER / mSCAPE / Biothreat Radar, Valthos / Red Queen Bio, NTI + BioFirewall guardrail work), policy timeline (US Framework, EO 14292, 50-nt screening window due 2026-10-13), and a tiered assessment of where Helix.AI could contribute.
- FINDING (scope-recorded, not fixed): `docs/SAFETY_POLICY.md` documents dual-use/biosafety refusal categories (pathogen enhancement, bioweapon development, unethical germline editing, environmental harm) but none of it is enforced in code. Repo-wide search for `dual-use|biosafety|biosecurity|bioweapon` matches only under `docs/`. `backend/policy_checks.py` enforces agent-responsibility boundaries (infra leakage, plan mutation, scientific-intent drift) and is unrelated to biological hazard. Net effect: the platform relies entirely on the upstream LLM's own refusal behavior, logs nothing about near-miss requests, and produces no biosafety evidence trail.
  - Impact: not a release-gate blocker today (no threshold in `benchmarks/release_thresholds.yaml` covers it), but it makes committed documentation inaccurate and is a likely procurement blocker for any pharma or public-health biosafety review.
  - Suggested remediation if prioritized: a `biosafety_checks.py` gate at the `ExecutionBroker` boundary (the sole side-effect choke point), reusing the `PolicyViolationError` pattern, routing `flag_for_review` into the existing `orchestration/approval_policy.py` staging path, emitting a biosafety event into the existing trace format on every decision including allows, and failing closed like `StagingClassificationError`. Must ship with a legitimate-research benchmark measuring wrongful-refusal rate.
- RELATED PRIOR EVIDENCE (2026-07-03 entry above): `demo5.phylo.initial` already trips OpenAI's dual-use biology filter (400 `invalid_prompt`) on benign SARS-CoV-2 spike/variant content. Concrete in-house example that over-broad biosafety filtering degrades legitimate workflows, reinforcing that any gate we build needs a measured false-positive bound before it goes near the default path.

- ADDENDUM (same session, after full capability inventory): sharper version of the finding above. Helix.AI is not only an analysis platform — it has a live sequence-design and synthesis-ordering path: `mutate_sequence` (`tools/mutations.py`) -> `dna_vendor_research` (`tools/dna_vendor_research.py`) -> `synthesis_submission` (`tools/synthesis_submission.py`), plus `fetch_ncbi_sequence` (`tools/ncbi_tools.py`) which retrieves public pathogen sequences from GenBank/RefSeq with no accession blocklist. That chain executes today with zero sequence screening at any step, which is precisely the pathway the US Framework for Nucleic Acid Synthesis Screening and EO 14292 exist to govern.
  - Cheapest mitigation: register SecureDNA and/or IBBIS `commec` (both free) as tools via `tool_schemas.py` + `dispatch_tool()` and make screening a mandatory plan step on that path. NOTE for scoping: there is no MCP server endpoint in `main.py` today despite references in `docs/` and the `agent.py` header — integration is in-process, so do not scope this as MCP work.
  - Open question flagged for counsel: whether operating `synthesis_submission` puts us in scope of the Framework at all (it binds Providers and Manufacturers, but EO 14292 extends obligations toward non-federally-funded settings and adds enforcement). Decides whether screening is compliance-mandatory or merely good practice.
  - Second structural gap found: no authentication/authorization anywhere in `backend/` — no login, per-user API keys, RBAC, or multi-tenancy; rate limiting is by IP/anonymous identity. Blocks per-user audit attribution and any "who approved this" claim. Audit output today is `POLICY_AUDIT` JSON to stdout plus session JSON, with no immutable store.
  - Positive finding: more of the surveillance stack already exists than expected — `metagenomics_shotgun` (nf-core/taxprofiler), `metagenomics_16s` (nf-core/ampliseq), `genome_assembly` (nf-core/mag), `variant_calling` (nf-core/sarek) via `backend/nextflow_executor.py`, plus `bundle_generator.py` reproducibility ZIPs. Caveat: the Nextflow `aws_batch` tier is not implemented (local only, <=4 samples / <10 GB), which is on the critical path for any large-data surveillance use.- Autoloop stopped after max iterations: blockers: [{'id': 'working-tree-dirty', 'severity': 'high', 'description': 'Slices A–F changes are uncommitted. ## 2026-10-08 — P2: Secure Science pre-routing assessment (gate-flagged)

Implemented the Secure Science gate (`HELIX_SCIENCE_GATE_V1`): every staged plan
is assessed before an `ExecutionIntent` is built. Pure checks over
`(plan, objective, AssessmentContext)` (action classification, data sensitivity,
dual-use triage, sequence screening, identity) combine into one immutable
`SecurityAssessment` (outcome + policy envelope) persisted in the ledger.

- Outcomes: `DENY > REQUIRE_REVIEW > ALLOW_WITH_APPROVAL > ALLOW`. DENY → `DENIED`
  (terminal); REQUIRE_REVIEW → `WAITING_FOR_SECURITY_REVIEW`; neither builds an
  intent. REQUIRE_REVIEW is resolved by a `security_reviewer` via `SecurityReview`
  (approve → intent + `WAITING_FOR_APPROVAL`; deny → `DENIED`). Review is not
  approval — the normal human approval still follows.
- Fail-closed: broken check, missing screening adapter, or screening required but
  no sequence present → REQUIRE_REVIEW. `execute_plan=True` cannot bypass a held
  state. Dual-use triage is conservative routing to manual review, not a risk
  classifier (disclaimer added to `docs/SAFETY_POLICY.md`).
- New: `backend/security/` (assessor, checks, review_service), policies under
  `backend/config/security/policies/`, `backend/api/security.py`,
  `backend/contracts/security_review.py`, ledger assessment/review/audit kinds,
  checkpoint `WAITING_FOR_SECURITY_REVIEW`/`DENIED`, agent `SECURITY_ASSESSOR`.

Root-cause fixes found during P2:
- `_platform_stage_pending_plan` used `WorkflowCheckpoint` without importing it →
  NameError swallowed by the approval pre-gate `except`, letting a blocked plan
  execute. Added the import.
- `build_standard_response` rewrote blocked status to `needs_inputs` (due to
  `execute_ready=False`); added a guard to preserve `security_denied` /
  `security_review_required`.
- `SequenceScreeningCheck` returned ALLOW with no sequence to screen; now
  REQUIRE_REVIEW (`not_run`).

Verification: `tests/unit` → 1013 passed, 1 skipped, 5 pre-existing failures
(unchanged). New: `test_security_checks.py` (36), `test_execute_security_flow.py`
(6), ORCH-001 P2 steps un-skipped (security_assessment_with_envelope,
denied_state_blocks_intent). Schemas exported and mirrored to DataWeaver. New
benchmark case `benchmarks/cases/routing_safety/security-gate-no-bypass.yaml`.
Details: `artifacts/test_results/p2_secure_science_2026-10-08.md`.
